## Where the Framing Breaks Down

**The title says the slot was taken by the XQ-67A, and the record supports a narrower statement than
that.** Taken implies an act upon the X-67, and the article's finding is that nobody had to decide to
skip it. **The number 67 went to an unmanned allocation on 2023-07-19, and the X-67 became unrequestable
only when the X-68A was approved on 2025-08-20** \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]]
\[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]]. The vehicle consumed a number, and a later request closed a slot.

**The derivation from the research series is an inference and not a documented fact.** The compiler
asserts it with the phrase just like the X-58 and gives no reasoning
\[[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]]. The test in the passage headed The
Borrowing Claim Is Testable and It Passes supports it, with a tail probability of 0.03159 that the article
calls a weak signal on its own. **No document states that anybody chose 67 for that reason.**

**The analogy with the X-58 breaks at the officiality line.** The XQ-58A's description is official
Department wording and the XQ-67A's is not, so the phrase just like the X-58 joins a case the government
described to one it did not. That boundary is drawn under the heading The Second Case Falls on the Far
Side of a Line the First Did Not.

**The framing of the skip as visible at the article's date also breaks, and the dated note under Epistemic State records how.**
The X-68A row reached the public register only after this article's
date, so a reader of the register at that date would have seen 67 as the next number rather than as a
passed one.

**And the aeroplane crosses the boundary the opening draws.** The opening treats the XQ-67A as evidence
about the number and not as the subject, yet the part-count, unit-cost, sharing-penalty, break-even and
refresh derivations are about the aeroplane's method. **Every input to them is assumed**, and the article
states that their yield is the shape of an argument rather than a measurement.

## The Source Base

**This article rests on a narrow base and the section exists to say how narrow.** Everything about the
designation comes from one compiler's reconstruction and one published instruction. Everything about the
aeroplane comes from two press releases and the federal award record. **No document in either group was
written to answer the question this article asks.**

**The research survey draws on three archives, being the National Aeronautics and Space Administration's
Technical Reports Server, the Defense Technical Information Center and the Crossref bibliographic index,
called below the reports server, the defence registry and the bibliographic index.** It holds 1,109
research records, being 1,105 from those archives and four hand-written theory sources, of
which 130 are report primaries, being 11.7 percent, by the identifier rule set out under
The Definition of Primary. **The median publication year is 2011.** Of the 1,062 dated
records, 371 were published in 2015 or later, being 34.9 percent, and 181 before
2000, and the range runs from 1900 to 2026.

**The reference base as a whole is 1,198 definitions and it partitions four ways.**

$$
\lvert \mathcal{B} \rvert \;=\; \lvert \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{prim}} \rvert
+ \lvert \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{hand}} \rvert
+ \lvert \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{rel}} \rvert
+ \lvert \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{res}} \rvert
$$

**22 are hand-written references to the register, the instructions, the
releases, the award record and the trade press, two of which the list marks as secondary, four are
hand-written theory sources, 67 are the prior articles of this series, and 1,105 are research
records from the three archives.** **Of the 1,109 research records the 979 that are not reports carry
bibliographic-index identifiers**, which is the proportion the subject dictates rather than a choice.

### The Reports Server Holds Almost None of This

**The reports server's own commonality literature is mostly spacecraft**, as the `commonality` row of
the table in the next subsection shows, and six of the ten reports-server records in the
commonality cluster concern space vehicles, stations or spaceflight hardware. **How much two vehicles can share is the question the XQ-67A was built to
answer whatever the vehicles are**, so that government literature belongs to the subject, but the journal
literature on product families and modular architecture is far larger and the reports server holds almost
none of it.

**That is a finding about the subject.** Commonality and product family design are a manufacturing and
management literature, and a research agency that builds aeroplanes publishes about aeroplanes.
**Whether the laboratory's own programme documents exist in the report literature under other names is
open**, the Low Cost Attritable Aircraft Technologies initiative and the Low-Cost Attritable Strike
Demonstrator being two such names, together with the Broad Agency Announcement the trade coverage dates
to September 2020.

### The Programme's Own Words Are Unsearchable

**Every word the laboratory and the contractor use for the programme collides with another literature,
and searching the archives for each bare term shows it.**

| Bare term | Records the reports server reports | What the first ten actually were |
|---|---:|---|
| `platform` | 6,270 | a satellite bus, an information system, a sea-based calibration platform, a scan platform, a Venus lander |
| `aircraft platform sharing` | 19 | air traffic management and emergency response, because sharing there means sharing airspace information |
| `commonality` | 421 | space station commonality, lunar and Martian hardware commonality, Mars habitat commonality |
| `component commonality` | 41 | mostly Space Station Freedom, and one paper on a family of commuter airplanes |
| `modularity` | 327 | robotic systems, evolutionary design, solar power satellites, deep space habitation |
| `genus` | 173 | microbiology, and the genus curve of a cluster catalogue |
| `species` | 8,982 | chemical species, biological species, anatomical comparison across species |
| `chassis` | 176 | electronic packaging and vehicle frames, the closest being a rack for circuit boards |
| `common chassis aircraft` | 1 | one record, and it is an avionics architecture for a different vehicle |
| `OBSS` | 15 | the Space Shuttle's Orbiter Boom Sensor System, in all ten |
| `attritable` | 2 | two records, both the same wing structure |
| `attritable aircraft` | 0 | nothing |
| `designation` | 161 | selenographic coordinates, route designation, and one paper on classification systems for materials |

**The bibliographic index was no better and in two cases was worse.** There, `platform` returns platform
capitalism, platform urbanism, the platform economy, a media journal and an oligonucleotide synthesis
platform, with nothing engineering in ten. `commonality` returns a dictionary entry for the word itself,
commonality in tinnitus perception and commonality of beliefs in economics. **`genus species
classification` returns ten biological results in ten**, and `common chassis aircraft family` returns a
fire-fighting truck, a passenger-boarding lift, automotive chassis grease and a glider undercarriage.

**The acronym collision is the one worth recording for its own sake.** The programme's own abbreviation
was already taken, inside the same agency, by the Space Shuttle's Orbiter Boom Sensor System, and all ten
of the reports server's first results for it are Shuttle return-to-flight material. **A programme named
after a function will collide with every other programme named after a similar function**, and this
series has now met the same class in a carbohydrate and a bird.

**The literature's own vocabulary finds the subject where the programme's does not.** `product
family`, `component commonality`, `design structure matrix`, `modular open systems approach` and
`product platform design` return their own subject at close to ten in ten. **The
[X-66][related_post_a363_boeing_x66] article reports the same lesson in the opposite direction**, since
there the report literature's vocabulary was the aeronautical one and here it is not aeronautical at
all.

### An Address That Returns a 404 Page Under a 200 Status

**The contractor's page for its aircraft family is not a document.** It answers with a success status
and 20,974 bytes, and its body reads *404 Error, Page Not Found*. **A success status is not a
document**, and this page is a case in which the status and the content disagree.

**The consequence for the article is substantive rather than cosmetic.** Secondary coverage speculates
that the XQ-67A represents the first member of a named family of aircraft the contractor has disclosed
separately. Contemporary reporting names the contractor's submission as the Gambit and reports the
contractor saying that the selection would validate the Gambit series' design concept
\[[AFRL Chooses General Atomics Drone for Off-Board Sensing Station Program][ref_dp_obss_2023]].
**With the maker's own page unreachable, the article asserts no tie to the family beyond that report**,
which is what the record supports.

### What the Cited Addresses Return

**Every hand-written address in the article has been fetched, and there are 26 of them.** Twenty-one
return a document, four are held by a publisher that declines automated clients and are instead
confirmed against the bibliographic index by author and title, and one returns neither. The one that
returns neither is the Department's own cancelled list at its issuance portal, which answers HTTP 403 to
automated clients, and the article says in its own definition that it was not read
\[[DoD 4120.15-L][ref_dod_412015l_2018]]. **A fetch that fails tells a reader nothing about whether a
citation is correct**, so each of the four is resolved against the index and the resolved title and
authors are compared against the label the article prints, and all four agree.

### The Definition of Primary

**Primacy is a property of the document, and this survey derives it from the identifier.** A research
record is a report primary when its address is on the reports server or the defence registry, or when it
carries a digital object identifier under the 10.21236 prefix, which Crossref registers for the defence
registry's reports. The report-primary share is the fraction of research records that are report
primaries.

$$
\varpi \;=\; \frac{K_{\mathrm{reports}} + K_{\mathrm{defence}}}{K}
$$

Of the 1,109 research records, 130 are reports held by the reports server or the defence registry, so
$\varpi$ is **11.7 percent**, split 37 from the reports server and 93 from the
defence registry. **The rule does not reach the documents this article actually rests on**, being the
register, the instructions and the releases, which are hand-written references rather than research
records, so the share describes the survey and not the article's evidence about the X-67.

### Thin Subjects

**There is no literature on the X-67 and the article states that rather than implying coverage.** The
survey holds no record whose subject is this designation, because a designation that was never allocated
produces nothing. **The 1,109 research records map the field the question sits in and not the
question**, and the only sources that speak to the X-67 directly are the register, the compilation of
missing designations and the instruction.

**Six subjects that this article's derivations depend on have thin literatures.** Each count below is the
number of research records in this survey whose title carries at least one of the listed terms, with a
hyphen read as a space. Where a row joins two groups of terms with the word together, a title must carry a
term from each group.

| Subject | Title terms counted | Records |
|---|---|---|
| the X-67 and the aeroplane that took its number | X-67, XQ-67, XQ-67A, Off-Board Sensing Station, OBSS | 0 |
| the designation of military aircraft | designation, designations, designator, designators, Mission Design Series | 0 |
| a shared genus carrying several species | genus, species, chassis | 0 |
| commonality across a family of aircraft | aircraft, airplane, airplanes, aeroplane, airframe, airframes, airliner, transport, transports, unmanned aerial vehicle, unmanned aerial vehicles together with family, families, commonality, platform, platforms | 17 |
| technology refresh | technology refresh, refresh, technology insertion | 1 |
| the allocation of identifiers | allocation, allocating together with identifier, identifiers | 3 |

**The designation, the aeroplane and its metaphor have no literature at all.** No research title names
the X-67, the XQ-67A or the Off-Board Sensing Station, none names a designation system, and none carries
genus, species or chassis. **This is the same negative result the
[X-66][related_post_a363_boeing_x66] article reported for airport design and the
[X-65][related_post_a362_aurora_x65_crane] article for its effector**, and it has the same cause. **A
laboratory demonstrator whose purpose is a production method does not generate a research literature
under its own name.**

**Commonality across a family of aircraft is a small part of a large literature.** Of the 17 titles,
4 name an unmanned aerial vehicle and the other 13 concern crewed aircraft, most of
them airliners, transports and commuter aircraft, so the shared-chassis argument above draws on a
literature written about airliners and about products in general.

**Technology refresh appears in one title**, on technology insertion in
evolutionary acquisition, so the refresh derivation stands on its own relations rather than on a published
model of refresh cadence.

**The allocation of identifiers appears in three titles**, one of them the procedure by which a
registry allocates algorithm numbers for the security extensions of the domain name system, which is the
closest the survey comes to the designation system's own allocation question.

### What the Survey Admits

**A record is admitted only when a person reading its title finds it on this article's subject**, and
every admitted title also carries the characteristic phrases of at least one of the fifteen clusters, so
no residual cluster is needed.

**Many words this subject depends on also name something else, and a record that shares only such a word
is excluded.** Numerical taxonomy applied to plants, microorganisms, soils, wines and psychiatric
diagnoses is another science using the method, so its applications are excluded while its method and
theory are kept. Learning curve also names surgical, financial and trainee learning curves,
standardisation also names the standardisation of cheese, Ayurvedic preparations, herbal products,
surveying practice and medical devices, modularity also names the graph sense used in network science,
commonality also appears in the thermal and fluid sciences and names a statistical technique for finding
the shared tool behind defective semiconductor wafers, modular also names porous chemical frameworks and
cognitive architectures, namespace also names an isolation mechanism of an operating system kernel and the
declarations file of a software package, and demonstration also names a flight display team and an
experiment flown on a parabolic flight. Nomenclature of crystal chemistry and of chemical substances names
natural rather than engineered objects. Real options valued without an engineering object, including in
organisational design, are a finance technique and are excluded, and so are product variety and economies
of scale in trade theory. Records about a demonstrator or an unmanned aircraft that report only an
aerodynamic, thermal, aeroelastic, handling, flight-control or calibration result are excluded, because the
article uses demonstrators as programmes and not as test articles. Obituaries, conference session headers,
meeting programmes, figure records and supplementary files are excluded as well, because a survey counts
research works and those are not works or are parts of works.
Reviews of books, dictionary and other reference entries of two pages or fewer, news and other
notices, and a second deposit of a document already in the survey are not admitted either, while an authored
encyclopedia article of more than two pages and a textbook chapter are research works and stay.

**The theory of modularity is admitted by its own distinctive phrases**, because its foundational works
state the theory rather than an application of it and carry no engineering noun. **Product modularity is
admitted as a phrase in its own right for the same reason**, since a core reference on its measures and
design methods pairs the word with no engineering noun either
\[[Gershenson et al. 2004][research_product_modularity_measures]]. The
cognitive, neural and graph senses of the word are excluded.

**Some records of doubtful relevance are kept.** Methodological papers on numerical taxonomy, identifier
registries outside engineering such as the Legal Entity Identifier and a statewide student identifier
system, learning curves in energy production, the nomenclature standards of engineering disciplines, and
modular architecture applied to buildings, recipes and services each share a method or an
information-infrastructure sense with this subject, so each is kept and a reader may weigh it accordingly.

**Every research title has been read for relevance, so the off-topic share that remains is a matter of
reading judgement.** A reading of titles cannot show that a work is relevant in substance, so this is a
statement about titles only.

## Epistemic State

**This article's claims divide unusually sharply, because its primary sources are a table and an
instruction and almost everything else is computed from them.** One division below,
Measured From the Documents Rather Than Asserted by Them, holds claims that are measurements of a
document rather than assertions by it.

### The Article's Own Vantage Point

**This article carries the editorial date 2025-12-12 and three of its statements are dated after
that.** All three are measurements the article made of itself rather than events in the subject. The
register is read from its page as last updated on 23 August 2026, the hostname check against the
authoritative nameservers was run on 2 October 2026, twice, and the reference survey's year range reaches
2026 because the bibliographic index carries records published after the dateline.

**A survey is written from current knowledge, and this section says where that knowledge postdates
the article's own date.** **No event
in the subject is dated after the dateline.** The register's latest allocation used here is
2025-11-21, the aeroplane's latest flight test is July 2025, and the latest instruction is of
3 November 2020.

**The one place this matters to a reader is the hostname.** A name that did not resolve on 2 October 2026
may have resolved on 2025-12-12 and may resolve later. **The article's claim is about the date it was
checked and not about the whole interval**, and no other claim here could change without any
document changing.

**A note on the register.** The X-68A and X-76A rows first appeared in the public register between 15 January and 1 February 2026, after this article's date. The register's page as archived on 10 December 2025 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 10 December 2025][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2025_12]\] and on 15 January 2026 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 15 January 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_01]\] ends its research rows at the X-66A, and the page as archived on 1 February 2026 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 1 February 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_02]\] carries both new rows. **In the register, the passed number at 67 is visible only through the X-68A row**, so a reader of the register at this article's date would have seen the X-67 as the next number rather than as a passed one. The skip is therefore a fact that the register made public after this article's date, and the statement above that no event in the subject is dated after the dateline holds for the allocations and not for their publication.

### Historical Fact, From Primary Documents

- **The designation XQ-67A was allocated on 2023-07-19 to General Atomics, with
  USAF as sponsor and an engine entry of 1 Williams FJ44-4A** \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]].
- **The X-66A was allocated on 2023-05-03 and the X-68A on 2025-08-20**, the latter to
  General Atomics \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]].
- **The XQ-58A was allocated on 2017-07-10 to Composite Engineering** \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]].
- **The XQ-67A first flew on 2024-02-28 at the General Atomics Gray Butte Flight Operations
  Facility near Palmdale, California** \[[AFRL first flight release][ref_afrl_firstflight]]
  \[[GA-ASI first flight release][ref_gaasi_firstflight]].
- **The contractor was selected in 2021 to design, build and fly the aircraft** and the laboratory
  initially worked with five industry vendors \[[GA-ASI first flight release][ref_gaasi_firstflight]]
  \[[AFRL first flight release][ref_afrl_firstflight]].
- **The federal award record carries two Off-Board Sensing Station contracts, of 67,986,112.00 dollar and
  16,003,302.00 dollar, beginning 2021-10-26 and 2021-10-25**, and
  five platform-sharing contracts totalling 24,479,631.19 dollar awarded in spring 2019
  \[[USAspending][ref_usaspending]].
- **In July 2025 the aeroplane flew with government reference autonomy and an active tactical datalink**
  \[[GA-ASI autonomy release][ref_gaasi_autonomy_2025]].
- **The governing instruction defines the next-in-series designator from the last approved design number
  of the same basic mission, refuses requests in reverse or skipped sequences, and reserves to the
  allocating office the authority to skip a design number at discretion**
  \[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].
- **The founding document of 18 September 1962 defines the design number as the sequence number of each
  new design of the same basic mission, gives three worked examples of changes requiring a new design
  number, and states that the requesting service names the mission while the assignment agency assigns
  the number** \[[AFR 66-11 and AR 700-26 and BUWEPS 13100.7, 18 September 1962][ref_afr_66_11_1962]].
- **That document also defines the X as both a status prefix meaning experimental and a basic mission
  symbol meaning research, and defines the Q as a modified mission symbol meaning drone** rather than as
  a basic mission \[[AFR 66-11 and AR 700-26 and BUWEPS 13100.7, 18 September 1962][ref_afr_66_11_1962]].
- **It publishes the next research design number as 22A**, in a list of four current research aircraft
  \[[AFR 66-11 and AR 700-26 and BUWEPS 13100.7, 18 September 1962][ref_afr_66_11_1962]].
- **The editions of 1994, 1997 and 2005 all direct the coordinating office to assign the next available
  consecutive design number, and none contains the word skip**
  \[[AFJI 16-401, 9 September 1994][ref_afji_16_401_1994]]
  \[[AFJI 16-401, 1 September 1997][ref_afji_16_401_1997]]
  \[[AFI 16-401(I), 14 April 2005][ref_afi_16_401_2005]].
- **The edition of 16 May 2014, which supersedes the 2005 one, defines the next designator from the last
  approved design number, refuses requests in skipped sequences for vehicles with an extensive history of
  designations, and states that skipped design numbers cannot be requested or used**, and it states that
  DoD 4120.15-L will no longer be maintained as the primary source of approved designators
  \[[AFI 16-401, 16 May 2014][ref_afi_16_401_2014]].
- **The Department directive of 29 November 2004, as amended by Change 2 of 17 September 2020, requires a
  unique designator for each vehicle and annual publication of a single Department-wide source document,
  and states no rule on choosing or skipping a design number**
  \[[DoD Directive 4120.15, 29 November 2004][ref_dodd_4120_15_2004]].
- **The 2020 instruction cancels DoD 4120.15-L as the publicly accessible database, names `data.af.mil`
  as where the public may obtain the latest version, and adds a status prefix `e` for aircraft engineered
  in a virtual environment** \[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].
- **Change 1 of 31 August 2018 to DoD 4120.15-L reassigns the office of primary responsibility and does
  nothing else**, the words retire, cancel and the successor address appearing nowhere in it
  \[[DoD 4120.15-L, 2004 with Change 1][ref_dod_412015l_2004]].
- **The October 1998 edition is approved for public release with distribution unlimited and gives three
  routes to a copy** \[[DoD 4120.15-L, October 1998][ref_dod_412015l_1998]].
- **The 2004 edition lists the X-41A, the X-42A and the X-43A as approved designators**
  \[[DoD 4120.15-L, 2004 with Change 1][ref_dod_412015l_2004]].
- **The instruction defines the design number as identifying major design changes within the same basic
  mission, and the series letter as identifying major modifications that significantly alter systems or
  components or change the logistics support of the vehicle** \[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].

### Measured From the Documents Rather Than Asserted by Them

**This division exists because the article's central claims are counts over a table.** Each of these was computed by parsing the register and each can be recomputed by anyone who
parses it the same way.

- **The register carries 539 designation rows, 532 of them well formed, and of the well-formed rows
  503 carry a full date and 29 a partial one, spanning 1997-12-10 to
  2025-11-21.** Of the 539 rows, 103 carry the compiler's mark of some kind.
- **There are 31 research-series rows carrying 25 distinct design numbers from
  37 to 76, and fifteen of the 40 positions in that span carry no row.**
- **The count of research-series rows carrying the design number 67 is zero.**
- **21 research rows are fully official, seven have an unofficial description,
  one is partly unofficial and two are entirely unofficial rows.** The XQ-58A's
  description is official and the XQ-67A's is not.
- **Six pairs of design numbers in the register carry identical official descriptions,   and in five of them the contractor is the only differing field.**
- **27 design numbers carry rows naming more than one leading firm, and the missile design
  number 88 carries ten series letters across seven firms.**
- **The research series has two companions in the unmanned series carrying the experimental
  prefix and nothing else, being XQ-58A and XQ-67A.**
- **The unmanned series carries nine out-of-sequence design numbers against a sequential ceiling of
  31, with numbers reaching 170.**
- **The hostname the cancellation notice named as successor to the printed list returns no answer from   the authoritative nameservers for its zone**,
checked on 2 October 2026, while a sibling name in
  the same zone and a name in an adjacent zone both resolve.

### Engineering Analysis, Derived Here and Checkable

- **The number-and-series field of one basic mission holds 23,976 distinct codes, rising to
  239,976 when the design-number range expands as the instruction provides.**
- **A sequential allocation carries no information beyond its order, and a chosen one carries at most   9.8657 bits at the research series' position in July 2023.**
- **The departures of the dated out-of-sequence unmanned allocations are 5 for the number 35, 17 for the number 48, 30 for the number 58, 37 for the number 67, 44 for the number 72 and 43 for the number 73.** Their entropy is
  2.5850 bits, which equals the maximum 2.5850 attainable at
  six observations, **so the entropy figure is pinned by sample size and is not a result.**
- **The distinct-part ratio of a family is one over the species count plus the complement of that times
  the unshared fraction**, giving 0.6000 at three species and a shared fraction of three fifths and
  0.3333 in the limit of full sharing.
- **With an eighty-five percent progress ratio the learning exponent is 0.23447**, and the
  fleet-average cost factor at three species and a shared fraction of three fifths is 0.86375, a
  saving of 13.63 percent.
- **With a sharing penalty linear in the shared fraction the optimum is always a corner**, and full
  commonality beats none when the penalty coefficient is below 29.38 percent at three species.
- **With a sharing penalty growing as the square of the shared fraction an interior optimum exists only
  when the penalty coefficient exceeds three times the square of the sharing slope**, which is
  15.47 percent at three species. **At a coefficient of 30 percent the optimum
  shared fraction is 44.63 percent, saving 4.76 percent, while full commonality is
  0.48 percent worse than sharing nothing.**
- **The time-average capability shortfall of a design frozen for an interval is one minus the ratio of
  one less the exponential of minus the growth-rate-interval product to that product**, giving
  37.82 percent at three years and 80.87 percent at fifteen on a two-year doubling.
- **A chassis costing the whole platform-sharing programme repays itself over two species only if a
  bespoke demonstrator would have cost 80,225,927.59 dollar**, a premium of 18.00 percent over
  what the first species cost.

### Inference, Stated as Such

- **That the number 67 was taken from the research series is an inference, now supported by   evidence the register's compiler does not cite.**
The number equalled the research series' next
  number at the moment of allocation, it equalled no other series' next number, and the same test
  fires for the only other case the compiler derives this way. **The inference is strong but   not a documented fact.**
- **That the X-68A's approval made the X-67 unrequestable** follows from the instruction's
  refusal of requests in skipped sequences, applied to the register's dates. **The instruction is a
  fact, the dates are facts, and the conjunction is an inference.**
- **That the design number indexes a request rather than a design or a designer** is an inference from
  the identical-description pairs and the multi-firm numbers. **Both measurements are facts and the
  conclusion drawn from them is not.**
- **That a species on a shared genus falls below the instruction's threshold for a series letter** is
  an inference from the logistics-support clause and the article's most contestable claim.
- **That the designation arriving mid-build is the ordinary case for a modern demonstrator** is an
  inference from one case and should be treated as a hypothesis the remaining articles can test.
- **That the laboratory's account of deciding at the end of 2021 is most likely an error for the end of
  2022 or a reference to the award itself** is an inference from a twelve-month base period, and the
  record contains nothing that chooses.
- **That a species on a shared chassis would not have earned a new design number under the 1962 test** is
  an inference from three worked examples, all of which concern the airframe's shape or its propulsion
  and none of which concerns mission equipment. **The examples are facts and the application is not.**
- **That the 1997 wording permitted the X-49A's fill** is an inference from a procedural duty on the
  assigning office rather than from a stated permission to a requester.

### What the Record Does Not Settle

- **Who caused the X-67 to be skipped, or whether anybody did.** The three readings in the body differ in
  agency and agree in every observable. **The record contains no allocation letter, no refusal, no
  exercise of the discretion clause and no statement of intent.**
- **Why the number 67 rather than any other.** The compiler's derivation is an analogy to the earlier
  case, the test in this article corroborates it, and **no document says that anyone chose it for that
  reason.**
- **Whether the design-number pool is shared across basic missions in practice.** The instruction says it
  is not. The register's behaviour is consistent with its being so. **No publication states the practice.**
- **Whether the successor designation database exists and is maintained.** The address does not resolve
  publicly and a Department network may resolve it. **This article cannot see inside.**
- **What the earlier Department directives behind the instructions say.** DoD Directive 4505.6 of
  6 July 1962, which the founding regulation implements, and DoD Directive 4120.15 of 2 May 1985, which
  the 1994, 1997 and 2005 instructions implement and the lists are reissued under, are
  **named in documents this article has read and have not themselves been read.** Their successor of 29 November 2004
  has been read as amended in 2020, and it states no numbering rule \[[DoD Directive 4120.15, 29 November 2004][ref_dodd_4120_15_2004]].
- **What the Off-Board Sensing Station cost to develop, or what any part of the genus cost.** The award
  record gives contract amounts and the releases give none, and no document separates the chassis from
  the species.
- **Whether a second species was ever built.** The off-board weapon station is described as a concept the
  programme wanted to design and no such aircraft appears in the register as last updated on 23 August 2026.
- **Whether the aeroplane is part of the family its maker has disclosed separately.** Contemporary
  reporting names the maker's submission as the Gambit and reports the maker saying that the selection
  would validate the Gambit series' design concept
  \[[AFRL Chooses General Atomics Drone for Off-Board Sensing Station Program][ref_dp_obss_2023]],
  **and the maker's page for that family returns a not-found document under a success status.**
- **How many species a genus would need to carry before the approach paid.** The relation is derived here
  and every input to it is unpublished.

## Out of Scope

**Each exclusion below is a thing a reader could reasonably look for here and not find.**

- **The airframe's performance.** No span, weight, thrust, endurance or speed appears above, because the
  register records none of them and the releases record none of them. **The aeroplane enters this article
  as evidence about a design number and the article declines to assemble a specification from trade
  coverage.**
- **Attritability and the arithmetic of losing aircraft.** The
  [X-58][related_post_a355_x58_slot_taken_by_xq58] article derived the cost per sortie, the break-even
  survival probability and the dependence on a cost ratio, and showed where the argument fails. **That
  material is not repeated and the cluster in the survey is deliberately small.**
- **The collaborative combat aircraft programme.** The register carries the YFQ-42A, the YFQ-44A and the
  YFQ-48A, all out of sequence, and the compiler's derivations for them appear above only as entries in
  a table. **Those aircraft are a different subject and two of them are not in the research series at
  all.**
- **The X-68A.** It appears here as the allocation whose approval closed the slot and as a date. **It is   the next article in this series, and its programme, its contractor's other work and its anomalies   are treated there.**
- **Autonomy and crewed-uncrewed teaming as technical subjects.** The 2025 flight test is cited for what
  it shows about the aeroplane's role as a testbed. **The autonomy stack, the datalink and the teaming
  concept are a literature this article surveys and does not analyse.**
- **The wider history of the designation system.** The 1962 unification, the pre-1962 service systems and
  the popular-name rules are reached only where the instruction's current text requires. **The system's   history is left to the closing article of this series.**
- **The platform and commonality literature as an engineering discipline**, including commonality
  indices with competing definitions, design structure matrix clustering and option-valuation methods.
  **The survey points at that literature and the article does not summarise it.**
- **Whether the genus and species approach is a good idea.** The article derives what it would have to be
  worth and reports that none of the inputs is public. **It takes no position.**

## Conclusion

**The X-67 does not exist, the number 67 does, and the register records the second with an analogy to the
case before it.** The compiler's entry says that just like the X-58 the slot was skipped after the
allocation of the XQ-67A, and that sentence is all the public reasoning there is
\[[Missing USAF and DOD Aircraft Designations][ref_missing_mds]].

**The analogy is sound and the article had to supply the evidence for it.** The number 67 was the
research series' next number on the day the unmanned allocation took it, the number 58 was the research
series' next number on the day the earlier one took it, and across six out-of-sequence unmanned
numbers tested against twenty basic missions the test fires two times and names the
research series both times \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]]. **The compiler asserts the
derivation and declines to say why. The register he compiled contains the reason.**

**What the earlier article could not say is who skipped it, and the instruction turns out to say that the
question is malformed.** The next designator to request is defined from the last approved design number,
and requests in reverse or skipped sequences are not accepted
\[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]]. **Nobody had to decide to skip the X-67. Somebody had to ask for
the X-68**, which happened on 2025-08-20, 763 days after the borrowing, and from
that approval the X-67 became unrequestable. **A skipped number is not a vacancy in this system. It is a
position behind a pointer that only moves forward.** The rule entered the instruction with the issue of
16 May 2014 \[[AFI 16-401, 16 May 2014][ref_afi_16_401_2014]], so the X-58's slot had already closed the same way,
**and the X-67 is the second research number made unrequestable by it.**

**The second case differs from the first in the one respect a register exists to record.** In 2017 the
government stated what the aeroplane taking the number was for, and the register carries that statement
as official wording. **In 2023 it did not, and the register's description of the XQ-67A carries the mark
saying the words are the compiler's.** The date, the contractor and the engine remain official. **So the
government will say officially which engine is in the aeroplane that consumed the X-67 and will not say
what the aeroplane is for**, and the compiler's phrase just like the X-58 reaches across that boundary.

**And the aeroplane that consumed it was built to deny the premise the number rests on.** A design number
marks a major design change within a basic mission, which presumes that an aeroplane has one design
identity. **The Off-Board Sensing Station existed to show that it has two levels, a shared genus and a
replaceable species**, and the laboratory said so in those words
\[[AFRL first flight release][ref_afrl_firstflight]]. **By the instruction's own criterion a species
belongs on a series letter**, because the only concrete test the instruction offers for that level is
whether the change alters the logistics support of the vehicle, and a common chassis is built so that it
does not.

**The founding document is harder on the point than the instruction now in force.** On 18 September
1962 the Department defined the design number as **the sequence number of each new design** and gave
three worked examples of what forces one, being a change in the number of engines, a change of the wing
from straight to swept or delta, and a change or relocation of the empennage
\[[AFR 66-11 and AR 700-26 and BUWEPS 13100.7, 18 September 1962][ref_afr_66_11_1962]]. **All three are
the airframe and none is the mission equipment.** A genus holds those three constant and swaps
what the 1962 test ignores. **The system that wrote the definition would have refused a species a
number of its own, and the system that inherited the definition without the examples gave one.**

**The register shows the same thing from the other side and shows it twice.** Six pairs of
design numbers carry identical official descriptions, and in five of them the
contractor is the only field that differs, so the number does not index the stated purpose. **Yet
27 design numbers survive a change of leading firm, one of them across
seven firms and ten series letters, so the number does not index the designer
either.** **What it indexes is a request**, and the instruction never defines what a request is for.

**The arithmetic of the method the aeroplane proved shows where its value would have to lie, and that place is not the parts.**
By the cost factor $\kappa$ under What It Does to a Unit Cost, at three species and an
eighty-five percent learning curve a shared fraction of three fifths buys 13.63 percent of unit cost. By
the threshold $\eta_{\ast}$ under The Omission Is the Penalty, a sharing penalty rising as the square of
the shared fraction has an interior optimum only above 15.47 percent, at which point full
commonality can be worse than none. **By the shortfall $\sigma$ under Why Frequent Refresh Is Worth
Something, shortening a design freeze from fifteen years to three takes the capability shortfall from
80.87 percent to 37.82 percent.** **If the
genus concept is worth what the laboratory claims, the value is in the cadence and not in the
commonality**, and every input to both calculations is unpublished.

**One institutional fact, larger than the designation, closes the case.** The request procedure
requires the requester to research the last approved design number in an official source. **The same
instruction that states that procedure cancels the publicly accessible list and directs the public to
`data.af.mil`** \[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]], **and that hostname returns no answer from the
authoritative nameservers for its own zone.** The public source left is one private compiler's
reconstruction obtained under the Freedom of Information Act, which reports the next research number as
69 while the instruction read literally gives 77 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]].

**Measured against what the system used to publish, that is a long fall.** The founding regulation
required the assignment agency to publish, not less frequently than every six months, an unclassified
listing of assigned designations carrying the complete designation, the responsible service and a short
unclassified description, and the October 1998 list gave three routes to a copy. The 2020 instruction
gives one address, and it does not exist
\[[AFR 66-11 and AR 700-26 and BUWEPS 13100.7, 18 September 1962][ref_afr_66_11_1962]]
\[[DoD 4120.15-L, October 1998][ref_dod_412015l_1998]] \[[DAFI 16-401][ref_dafi_16_401_2020]].

**So every public claim about the X-67, including every claim in this article, passes through a document
the Department did not publish.** The designation is empty, the emptiness is irreversible, the mechanism
is in the instruction, **and the only place a reader can see the gap at all is a table a private citizen
rebuilt because the official one was withdrawn.**
